0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\lambda\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\lambda\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\hfil$\crcr}}}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\omega)=\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle q\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle q\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle q\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle q\hfil$\crcr}}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}(\omega)\quad\text{with}\quad q^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{\ell}(\omega)=\int_{\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}}\hat{g}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x},\omega){\sigma}_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}},

(6.13)

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\ell k}(\omega)=16\pi^{2}(c^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}c^{\varepsilon}_{k})^{-1}\iint_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}\times\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k}}G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}},

where
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k}
denotes a sphere centered at
\boldsymbol{c}_{k}
with the radius
(4\pi)^{-1}c^{\varepsilon}_{k}
.

To state the key result of this section, let us define a new geometry. Let
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\varepsilon}_{k}=B(\boldsymbol{c}_{k},\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})
,
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k}:=(4\pi)^{-1}c_{k}^{\varepsilon}=(4\pi)^{-1}c_{k}^{1}\varepsilon
(see (
4.20
)).

Proposition 6.1
.

The following holds true:

1.

the equilibrium densities
\widetilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}_{k}
for
\widetilde{\Omega}_{k}^{\varepsilon}
are constant and equal to
(\rho_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}
;

2.

the capacitances of the obstacles
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\varepsilon}_{k}
are equal;

3.

if
(\Omega^{\varepsilon})_{0<\varepsilon\leq 1}
is admissible (see Definition
2.1
), so is
(\widetilde{\Omega}^{\varepsilon})_{0<\varepsilon\leq 1}
, and

\operatorname{min}\operatorname{dist}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}^{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}^{\varepsilon})\geq\operatorname{min}\operatorname{dist}({\Gamma}_{j}^{\varepsilon},{\Gamma}_{k}^{\varepsilon})=d^{\varepsilon}_{*},\quad\text{ for all }0<\varepsilon\leq 1.

Proof.

The first two statements follow immediately once we recall that the capacitance of the ball of radius
r
is
4\pi r
; this latter result follows from the expression

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B(0,r)}\frac{1}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}=r,\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\partial B(0,r)

and thus the associated equilibrium density equals
\sigma(\boldsymbol{x})=r^{-1}
.

To prove the third statement, by
[
24
, Exercise 8.12, p. 272]
,
c_{k}^{\varepsilon}\leq 4\pi\operatorname{diam}\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}
, thus
\rho_{k}^{\varepsilon}\leq\operatorname{diam}\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}
, which shows that
B(\boldsymbol{c}_{k},\rho_{k}^{\varepsilon})\subset B(\boldsymbol{c}_{k},\varepsilon r_{k})
, cf. (
2.2
). Then
(\widetilde{\Omega}^{\varepsilon})_{0<\varepsilon\leq 1}
is admissible since
(\Omega^{\varepsilon})_{0<\varepsilon\leq 1}
is, see the discussion after Definition
2.1
.
∎

The next proposition shows that, on one hand
K^{\varepsilon}_{s}
is essentially
K^{\varepsilon}_{G}
written for
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\varepsilon}
, and, on the other hand, that these operators are close as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
in a certain sense.

Proposition 6.2
.

For
K^{\varepsilon}_{s,\ell k}
defined in (
2.17
), it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}\rho_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{\delta(t-|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}\right)=\mathcal{F}\left(K^{\varepsilon}_{s,\ell k}\right)(\omega)=\mathbb{M}_{s,\ell k}^{\varepsilon}(\omega)

(6.14)

with

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\ell k}(\omega)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}4\pi i\omega(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})^{2}j_{0}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})h^{(1)}_{0}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k}),&k=\ell,\\
4\pi i\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}j_{0}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})j_{0}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})h^{(1)}_{0}(\omega|\boldsymbol{c}_{k}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|),&k\neq\ell,\end{array}\right.

where
j_{0}
,
h^{(1)}_{0}
denote the spherical Bessel and Hankel functions of the first kind.

For all
0<\varepsilon<1
, all
\omega\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, the matrix
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\omega)
is invertible, and the following two estimates hold true:

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon^{-1}C_{s,\operatorname{geom}}(N,{\underline{d}}^{\varepsilon}_{*})p_{s}(|\omega|,\operatorname{Im}\omega),

(6.17)

\displaystyle\|\left(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{-1}-(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon{C}_{\operatorname{diff},\operatorname{geom}}(N,\underline{{d}}_{*}^{\varepsilon}){p}_{\operatorname{diff}}(|\omega|,\operatorname{Im}\omega),

(6.18)

where
\|.\|_{F}
is the Frobenius matrix norm.

Proof.

Proof of (
6.14
)-(
6.2
).

We start by rewriting

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}\rho_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{\delta(t-|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}\right)

\displaystyle=(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})^{-1}\iint_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}\times\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k}}G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}

\displaystyle=(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})^{-1}(\widetilde{S}^{\varepsilon,\ell k}_{\omega}1,1)_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k})},

(6.19)

where we use the
\widetilde{.}
to indicate that the corresponding operator is defined on
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}
rather than
\Gamma^{\varepsilon}
:

(\widetilde{S}^{\varepsilon}_{\omega}\boldsymbol{\varphi})(\boldsymbol{x})=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}}G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)\varphi_{k}(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}},\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}.

Case
\ell=k
.

For the unit sphere, the spherical harmonics
\{Y^{m}_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N},~m=-n,\ldots,n\}
are eigenfunctions of the single layer boundary operator for the Helmholtz problem, i.e.

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}^{\varepsilon,kk}_{\omega}Y^{m}_{n}=\lambda_{n}Y^{m}_{n}\quad\text{with}\quad\lambda_{n}=i\omega(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})^{2}j_{n}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})h^{(1)}_{n}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k}),

where
j_{n}
and
h^{(1)}_{n}
are the spherical Bessel and Hankel functions of the first find, receptively, see
[
2
, p. 437, Section 10]
for details. Using this spectral representation and the orthogonality of spherical harmonics over the unit sphere, only
Y^{0}_{0}=(4\pi)^{-1/2}
contributes when integrating against a constant. Thus, inserting the above identity into (
6.19
) yields the equiality of (
6.19
) for
\ell=k
with
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s,kk}
.

Case
\ell\neq k
.

We make use of the Addition Theorem (cf.
[
2
, §10.1, p. 440, Formulas 10.1.45 and 10.1.46]
)

\displaystyle\frac{e^{i\omega|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}=\frac{i\omega}{4\pi}\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}(2n+1)j_{n}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})h^{(1)}_{n}(\omega|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|)P_{n}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}}),

(6.20)

for
\boldsymbol{x}\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}
,
\boldsymbol{y}\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k}
, with
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}=|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|
. Here
P_{n}
,
n\geq 0
are the Legendre polynomials.
Using the Addition Theorem (
6.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle(\tilde{S}^{\varepsilon,\ell k}_{\omega}1)(\boldsymbol{y})=\int_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}}G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}=\frac{i\omega}{4\pi}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}(2m+1)j_{m}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})h^{(1)}_{m}(\omega|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|)\int_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}}P_{m}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}},\quad\boldsymbol{y}\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k}

(6.21)

In order to compute the integral in the right-hand side of the above identity, we employ the Funk-Hecke formula, see
[
18
]
: for
f\in L^{2}(-1,1)
, it holds true the following identity

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f(\boldsymbol{x}\cdot\boldsymbol{y})Y^{m}_{n}(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}=2\pi\int_{-1}^{1}f(t)P_{n}(t)dtY^{m}_{n}(\boldsymbol{x}),\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{S}^{2},

(6.22)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
m=-n,\ldots,n
where
\mathbb{S}^{2}
denotes the unit sphere.
Then, changing the variables in the integral in (
6.21
), namely,
\boldsymbol{\tau}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}}=(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})^{-1}(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell})
, and then applying the Funk-Hecke formula (
6.22
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}}P_{m}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}=(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}P_{m}(\boldsymbol{\tau}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=2\pi(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}P_{m}(t)dt=4\pi(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})^{2},

(6.23)

where in the last equality we made use of the fact that the Legendre polynomials are pairwise orthogonal with respect to the standard
L^{2}(-1,1)
inner product. Thus, we have

\displaystyle(\tilde{S}^{\varepsilon,\ell k}_{\omega}1)(\boldsymbol{y})=i\omega(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})^{2}j_{0}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})h^{(1)}_{0}(\omega|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|),\quad\boldsymbol{y}\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k}.

(6.24)

Next, using
h^{(1)}_{0}(z)=e^{iz}/z
(see
[
2
, p. 438, 10.1.11-10.1.12]
), we apply the Addition Theorem again to
h^{(1)}_{0}(\omega|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|)
:

\displaystyle h^{(1)}_{0}(\omega|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|)=\sum_{m=0}^{+\infty}(2m+1)j_{m}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})h^{(1)}_{m}(\omega|\boldsymbol{c}_{k}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|)P_{m}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{c}_{k}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}}).

Inserting the above identity into (
6.24
) and then testing it with a constant, it follows

\displaystyle(\tilde{S}^{\varepsilon,\ell k}_{\omega}1,1)_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k})}=i\omega(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})^{2}j_{0}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})\sum_{m=0}^{+\infty}(2m+1)j_{m}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})h^{(1)}_{0}(|\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|)\int_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k}}P_{m}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{c}_{k}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}.

Computing the above integral in a similar manner like in (
6.23
) leads

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})^{-1}(\tilde{S}^{\varepsilon,\ell k}_{\omega}1,1)_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}_{k})}=4\pi i\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k}j_{0}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{\ell})j_{0}(\omega\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})h^{(1)}_{0}(|\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|).

Let us now prove (
6.17
).
In virtue of Proposition
6.1
, the equilibrium density
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{\varepsilon}_{k}
considered on
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}
equals
(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k})^{-1}\boldsymbol{1}^{\varepsilon}_{k}
,
k=1,\ldots,N
. Let us denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\varepsilon}
the operator
\mathcal{I}^{\varepsilon}
considered on
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\varepsilon}:\,\mathbb{C}^{N}\rightarrow H^{-1/2}(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon}),\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{N}\frac{v_{k}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{k}}\boldsymbol{1}^{\varepsilon}_{k}.

Let
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
. Then, by definition of
\mathbb{M}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\omega)
, it holds that

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern to conclude that

0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}})_{\mathbb{C}^{N}}\right|

\displaystyle\geq C\varepsilon(\underline{{d}}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{2}(1+|\omega|)^{-2}\min(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{3})\|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}\|^{2}_{H^{-1/2}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\geq C\varepsilon(\underline{{d}}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{2}(1+|\omega|)^{-2}\min(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{3})\|\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}^{N}},

where the last inequality follows from (
5.1
). From the above and the equivalence of the induced
2-
matrix and Frobenius norm (
\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\|_{F}\leq N^{1/2}\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\|_{2}
), we obtain (
6.17
):

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\|_{F}\leq C^{-1}N^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1}(d^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}(1+|\omega|)^{2}\max(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{-3}).

(6.25)

Proof of (
6.18
).
We will prove this result by comparing the matrices
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathbb{M}_{s}^{\varepsilon}
. Recall that
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
6.12
).

Expansion of the entries of
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Let us expand the entries of the matrix
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}
in the powers of
\varepsilon
. We start with the diagonal entries, which we develop like in Section
4.4.2
, recall (
4.45
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{kk}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\langle S^{\varepsilon,kk}_{0}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}+\frac{i\omega}{4\pi}\int_{\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}},\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{1/2,-1/2,\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}}+\langle E^{\varepsilon,kk}(\omega)\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon},\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{1/2,-1/2,\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}}.

Since
S^{\varepsilon,kk}_{0}\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}=1
, and recalling that
\int_{\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}=c_{k}^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{kk}(\omega)-c_{k}^{\varepsilon}-\frac{i\omega}{4\pi}(c_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}|\leq\|E^{\varepsilon,kk}(\omega)\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\leq\varepsilon^{3}|\omega|^{2}\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}.

where the last bound follows from (
4.48
) . With
\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\lesssim 1
, see (
4.20
),

\displaystyle|\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{kk}(\omega)-c_{k}^{\varepsilon}-\frac{i\omega}{4\pi}(c_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}|\leq C_{kk}\varepsilon^{3}|\omega|^{2},

(6.26)

where
C_{kk}>0
depends on
\Gamma_{k}
only.

Next, let us consider the off-diagonal entries, which we expand by developing
(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y})\mapsto G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)
into the Taylor series around
(\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell},\,\boldsymbol{c}_{k}):

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell k}(\omega)=G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|)

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Gamma_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}\times\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x})\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}+\iint\limits_{\Gamma_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}\times\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}}R^{\varepsilon}_{\ell k}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y})\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x})\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}},

\displaystyle\text{ where }\quad|R^{\varepsilon}_{\ell k}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y})|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y})\in B_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}\times B_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{|\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}}G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|^{2}+|\nabla_{\boldsymbol{y}}G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|^{2}+|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon(\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})^{-2}(1+|\omega|),

where in the last inequality we used (
4.59
) and
\sqrt{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell}|^{2}+|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|^{2}}\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{\ell}^{2}+r_{k}^{2}}
.

With this we conclude that, with
C_{\ell k}
only dependent of
\Gamma_{\ell}
and
\Gamma_{k}
,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell k}-G_{\omega}(\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})c_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}c_{k}^{\varepsilon}|\leq C_{\ell k}\varepsilon^{3}(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}(1+|\omega|),

(6.27)

where we used
\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\leq|\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}
and
\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\lesssim 1
, see (
4.20
).

Expansion of the entries of
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s}
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.
We apply the results of Step 4.1 to the matrix
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\ell k}
, which is a particular case of
\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell k}
for the case when all obstacles are balls of the same capacitance as
\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}
; thus, using also Proposition
6.1
, item 3,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s,kk}(\omega)-c_{k}^{\varepsilon}-\frac{i\omega}{4\pi}(c_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}|\leq\widetilde{C}_{kk}\varepsilon^{3}|\omega|^{2},\\
&|\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\ell k}(\omega)-G_{\omega}(\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})c^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}c^{\varepsilon}_{k}|\leq\widetilde{C}_{\ell k}\varepsilon^{3}({\underline{d}}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}(1+|\omega|).\end{split}

(6.28)

Estimating
(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}-(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}
.
We start by rewriting

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{{M}}^{\varepsilon}_{s}+\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}=\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\left(\mathbb{I}+(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\right),\text{ so that }

\displaystyle(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}

\displaystyle-(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}=\left(\left(\mathbb{I}+(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{-1}-\mathbb{I}\right)(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}.

If
\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F}<1
, the standard Neumann series argument yields the bound:

\displaystyle\|\left(\mathbb{I}+(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{-1}-\mathbb{I}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F}(1-\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F})^{-1},

and it holds that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}-\left(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)^{-1}\|_{F}\leq(1-\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F})^{-1}\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\|^{2}_{F}\|\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F}.

(6.29)

This is the bound that we would like to use. However, as we will see later, it appears to be impossible to apply for high frequencies
\omega
, and thus we will have to resort to other types of bounds in this case. We start by examining how the condition
\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F}<1
can be ensured.

An estimate on
\|\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F}
.

Comparing (
6.28
), (
6.27
) and (
6.26
) yields, with
C>0
,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}_{kk}(\omega)|\leq C\varepsilon^{3}|\omega|^{2},\quad|\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell k}(\omega)|\leq C\varepsilon^{3}(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}(1+|\omega|),\quad\ell\neq k,\text{ so that }

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F}\leq CN\varepsilon^{3}(\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})^{-2}(1+|\omega|)^{2}.

’Low-frequency’ estimate.
In view of the above bound and (
6.25
), under the condition

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\|_{F}\|\mathbb{E}^{\varepsilon}\|_{F}

(6.30)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2}\times CN^{3/2}(\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})^{-4}(1+|\omega|)^{4}\max(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{-3})<c_{\operatorname{lf}}<1,

we have, by (
6.29
),

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}-\left(\mathbb{{M}}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)^{-1}\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon C(1-c_{\operatorname{lf}})^{-1}N^{2}\left(\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{-6}(1+|\omega|)^{6}\max(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{-6}).

(6.31)

Recall the condition (
6.30
), which ensures the validity of (
6.29
):

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\times CN^{3/2}(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-4}(1+|\omega|)^{4}\max(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{-3})<c_{\operatorname{lf}}<1.

(6.32)

Let us now argue that the bound analogous to (
6.29
) holds true also in the case when (
6.32
) does not hold, in other words, when

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-2}\leq c^{-1}_{\operatorname{lf}}CN^{3/2}(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-4}(1+|\omega|)^{4}\max(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{-3}),

(6.33)

or, loosely speaking,
|\omega|
is sufficiently large.

’High-frequency’ estimate.

We assume (
6.33
) and use the triangle inequality

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}-\left(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)^{-1}\|_{F}\leq\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}\|_{F}+\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s})^{-1}\|_{F}.

Using (
6.25
) and remarking that by exactly same argument the bound analogous to (
6.25
) holds as well for
\left(\mathbb{{M}}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}-\left(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)^{-1}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-1}N^{1/2}\widetilde{C}(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}(1+|\omega|)^{2}\max(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{-3}).

Next, we rewrite the above as

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}-\left(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\right)^{-1}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\times\varepsilon^{-2}N^{1/2}\widetilde{C}(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}(1+|\omega|)^{2}\max(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{-3})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\widetilde{C}c^{-1}_{\operatorname{lf}}CN^{2}(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-6}(1+|\omega|)^{6}\max(1,(\operatorname{Im}\omega)^{-6}),

(6.34)

where in the last bound we used the condition (
6.33
).

To deduce the desired bound (
6.18
), it is sufficient to combine the bound (
6.34
), valid under (
6.33
), and (
6.31
), valid under (
6.32
).
∎

). It holds that

and next estimate the right-hand side. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
4.20
), with
C_{\sigma}:=\max_{k=1,\ldots,N}\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\lesssim 1
,

\displaystyle\|\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern \displaystyle\leq C_{\sigma}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}(\omega)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}^{2}.

The desired bound follows from (
6.17
) along with (
6.2.5
).

(6.68)

To conclude, we use the fact that
\mathcal{I}^{\varepsilon}
behaves like an isometry, cf. (
5.1
).
∎

Remark 11
.

Using the stability bound (
6.1
) with the isomorphism
\mathcal{I}^{\varepsilon}
, the density
\hat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\varepsilon}_{s}
satisfies a stability bound of the type
(
6.1
)
. This implies the time-domain well-posedness of the simplified model (
2.17
), see the reasoning in Section
5
.

6.2.6
Frequency-domain error of the scattered field

Recall that the scattered field is obtained via (
6.2
), which we rewrite in the Fourier-Laplace domain as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\operatorname{app}}(\boldsymbol{x},\omega)&=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{i\omega|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}\hat{\lambda}_{s,k}^{\varepsilon}c^{\varepsilon}_{k}+\\
&+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{N}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k}-\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{p}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{i\omega|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|^{3}}(i\omega|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|-1)\hat{\lambda}_{s,k}^{\varepsilon},\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\Omega^{\varepsilon,c}.\end{split}

(6.69)

Let the operator
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\omega):\,\mathbb{C}^{N}\rightarrow L^{2}(\Omega^{\varepsilon,c})
be defined via

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern
