Lemma 6.1

(Approximating the volume operator)
.

Let
K\subset B^{c}
be a compact set. Then, there exists
C_{K}>0
, s.t. for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
\omega\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon}_{G}(\omega)-\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\omega)\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{N},L^{\infty}(K))}\leq\varepsilon^{3}\times C_{K}N^{1/2}(1+|\omega|)^{2}.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{x}\in K
and
\boldsymbol{y}\in\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}
. Expanding the integral kernel in the Taylor series around
\boldsymbol{c}_{k}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{e}^{i\omega|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{i\omega|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}\left(1+(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})\cdot\frac{(\boldsymbol{c}_{k}-\boldsymbol{x})}{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}\left(i\omega-\frac{1}{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}\right)\right)+

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2}R_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},

with the Taylor’s series remainder given by, for
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2
,

\displaystyle R_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})=\frac{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|-1}D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{\boldsymbol{y}}G_{\omega}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}+t(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})|)dt,\quad D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{\boldsymbol{y}}:=\frac{\partial^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}}{\partial\boldsymbol{y}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}.

By the definitions (
6.1
) of
c_{k}^{\varepsilon}
and
\boldsymbol{p}_{k}^{\varepsilon}
and using the above expansion, we arrive at

\displaystyle((\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon}_{G}(\omega)-\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\omega))\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}})(\boldsymbol{x})=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{N}\varphi_{k}\int_{\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2}R_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}.

Applying the
\ell^{2}
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to (
6.2.6
) yields

\displaystyle|((\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon}_{G}(\omega)-\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\omega))\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}})(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}\leq\|\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\varphi\hfil$\crcr}}}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}^{N}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|\mathbb{R}_{k}[\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}]|^{2},

with
\mathbb{R}_{k}[\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}]:=\int_{\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}}\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2}R_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}
. It is straightforward to see that, since for
|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2
, it holds that
|(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|\leq\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathbb{R}_{k}[\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}]|&\leq\|\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2}R_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x},\cdot,\boldsymbol{c}_{k})(\cdot-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\|\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon^{3}\max_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2}\max_{\boldsymbol{y}\in\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\left|R_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})\right|\|\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}.\end{split}

(6.95)

Next, we use that, with (
4.20
),
\|\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\lesssim 1
, and

\displaystyle\left|R_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})\right|\lesssim\min(1,\operatorname{dist}(\boldsymbol{x},B^{\varepsilon}_{k}))^{-3}(1+|\omega|)^{2}.

Inserting the above into (
6.95
), and the result into (
6.2.6
) yields the desired bound in the statement of the theorem.
∎

With the above lemma, we obtain the following counterpart of Theorem
4.2
, modulo the bounds on the data.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
K\subset B^{c}
be compact;
\omega\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
and
\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\omega)\in H^{5}(B)
. The error between
\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}
and
\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\operatorname{app}}
, defined in (
4.1
) and (
6.69
), is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}-\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\operatorname{app}}\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\leq C_{K}C_{\operatorname{geom}}(N,\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})\,p(|\omega|,\operatorname{Im}\omega)\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{H^{5}(B)},

where
C_{K}>0
depends on
K
.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{x}\in K
.
We start with (
6.70
). With the help of Lemma
4.5
used to bound
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon}(\omega)
and Lemma
6.1
to bound the last term in (
6.70
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x},\omega)-\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\operatorname{app}}(\boldsymbol{x},\omega)\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{K}C_{\operatorname{geom}}(N,\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})\,p(|\omega|,\operatorname{Im}\omega)

\displaystyle\times\big[\varepsilon\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{1,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{2,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{3/2}\big(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{1,\perp}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{2,\perp}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{3}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}+\varepsilon^{3}\|\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\lambda\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\lambda\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\lambda\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\lambda\hfil$\crcr}}}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\|_{\mathbb{C}^{N}}\big].

Then, by using
the bounds (
6.9
), (
6.10
), the bounds of Section
6.2.4
and of Corollary
6.1
(namely (
6.59
), (
6.1
)), we arrive at the following bound:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x},\omega)-&\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}_{s,\operatorname{app}}(\boldsymbol{x},\omega)|\leq C_{K}C_{\operatorname{geom}}(N,\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})\,p(|\omega|,\operatorname{Im}\omega)\times\\
&\times\left(\varepsilon^{1/2}|\!|\!|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon}-\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}|\!|\!|_{-1/2}+\varepsilon^{3/2}|\!|\!|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}|\!|\!|_{3/2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\right).\end{split}

(6.96)

Next, we employ a counterpart of Proposition
4.9
, namely, Proposition
D.1
, to bound the above in terms of
\hat{u}
.
∎

6.2.7
Proof of Theorem
2.1
for the model (
2.17
)

The well-posedness proof follows the same lines as the proof of Theorem
5.1
, cf. the statement of Corollary
6.1
and Theorem
4.1
.

To prove convergence, we proceed like in the proof of Theorem
2.1
for (
2.15
), see Section
5
, however, this time work with the estimate of Theorem
6.1
.

7
Numerical experiments

In all numerical experiments, we employ the convolution quadrature method for the time discretization (see
[
21
,
22
,
23
]
for details). As a reference solution, we use results obtained via the Galerkin BEM.

7.1
Convergence of the asymptotic models

This section provides numerical validation of the results established in Theorem
2.1
. We consider a configuration of
N=5
obstacles with cavities, positioned in the origin and in the vertices of a regular tetrahedron; see Figure
2
for the geometric setup.

Figure 2
:
Geometric configuration of the numerical experiments for
\varepsilon=0.5
. The obstacles are centred at
\boldsymbol{c}_{1}=(0,0,0),~\boldsymbol{c}_{2}=(-1,-1,1),~\boldsymbol{c}_{3}=(-1,1,1),~\boldsymbol{c}_{4}=(1,-1,-1),~\boldsymbol{c}_{5}=(1,1,1)
.

For the incident field, we use a modulated Gaussian pulse (MGP) given by

\displaystyle u^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{x},t)=\cos\left(\omega(t-\boldsymbol{x}\cdot\boldsymbol{d})\right)\,e^{-\sigma(t-\boldsymbol{x}\cdot\boldsymbol{d}-\mu)^{2}}.

(7.1)

with
\omega=2\pi,\sigma=3,\mu=3,\boldsymbol{d}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(1,-1,1)
. The simulations are carried out over the time interval
(0,T)
, with final time
T=13
. We vary the parameter
\varepsilon
and compare the reference solution
u^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0},t)
with the approximate scattered fields
u^{\varepsilon}_{\operatorname{app}}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0},t)
computed using the three models introduced in Section
2.3
. The scattered fields are measured at the observation point
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}=(-1,-1,-1)
. Figure
3
illustrates the absolute error of the scattered field for different values of
\varepsilon
and the temporal evolution of the scattered field.
The numerical results validate the theoretical result stated in Theorem
2.1
. In particular, we observe that the absolute error behaves as
O(\varepsilon^{3})
for both the asymptotic model (
2.15
) and the simplified model (
2.17
), while for the Born approximation (
2.18
), the observed absolute error is of order
O(\varepsilon^{2})
, consistent with theoretical expectations.

Figure 3
:
(Left) Time evolution of the scattered field for
\varepsilon=0.1
. (Right) Absolute error of the scattered field computed using the three models described in Section
2.3
: the Galerkin-Foldy-Lax (GFL), Simplified Galerkin-Foldy-Lax (SGFL), and Born models.

7.2
Stability under periodic perturbations

In the second experiment, we study the behavior of the scattered field under periodic perturbations. To this end, we consider
N=4
non-rotated ellipsoids with principal semi-axes
(0.5,0.5,1)
positioned in the vertices of the regular tetrahedron, see Figure
2
. For the incident field, we set

\displaystyle u^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{x},t)=\sin(2\pi(t-\boldsymbol{x}\cdot\boldsymbol{d}-3))\sigma(t-\boldsymbol{x}\cdot\boldsymbol{d}-3).

with
\sigma(x)=\frac{1}{1+e^{-2x}},\boldsymbol{d}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(1,-1,1)
.

Figure 4
:
Dependence of the scattered field on time for
\varepsilon=0.05
.

The simulations were made on the time interval
(0,T)
, for the final time
T=25
. The scattered fields are measured at the origin
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}=(0,0,0)
. Figure
4
illustrates dependence of the scattered fields on time.

7.3
Simplified Model vs Born Approximation

In our third experiment, we compare the scattered field computed using the simplified Galerkin-Foldy-Lax model and the Born approximation for a large number of particles. Specifically, we consider a geometric configuration consisting of
N=71
non-convex obstacles distributed on the surface of a sphere with radius
r=1.5
using the Fibonacci lattice method
[
16
, Section 3.1]
; see Figure
5
.

As the incident field, we again use the MGP (
7.1
) with the following parameters:

\displaystyle\omega=2\pi,\quad\sigma=100,\quad\mu=2,\quad\boldsymbol{d}=(1,-1,1)/\sqrt{3}.

The simulations are performed over the time interval
(0,T)
with
T=11
, and the observation point is set at
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}=(-1.5,-1.5,-1.5)
. The time evolution of the scattered field at this point is presented in Figure
5
. According to the theoretical estimates stated in Theorem
2.1
, the difference between the scattered fields produced by the simplified model and the Born approximation is of
O(\varepsilon^{2})
.
This estimate is confirmed by the numerical results shown in Figure
5
.

Figure 5
:
(Left) Geometric configuration from Section
7.3
with
\varepsilon=0.25
; (Top right) Time evolution of the scattered field at
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}=(-1.5,-1.5,-1.5)
for
\varepsilon=0.05
. (Bottom right) An absolute error between the scattered field obtained from the simplified and Born models, measured in the
L^{\infty}(0,T)
norm.

Appendix A
Scaling of norms

Domains
\mathcal{O}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}

Let
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a Lipschitz domain such that
\boldsymbol{0}\in\mathcal{O}
. For
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, let us introduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}:=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\boldsymbol{x}/\varepsilon\in\mathcal{O}\},\text{ so that }\mathcal{O}=\mathcal{O}^{1},\text{ and }|\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}|:=\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}.

(A.1)

The following results are immediate from the definitions of the corresponding norms and a scaling argument, and thus we leave their proofs to the reader.

Lemma A.1
.

Assume
\varphi\in C(\overline{\mathcal{O}})
, then

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{0}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}|\partial\mathcal{O}|^{1/2},

where
|\partial\mathcal{O}|=\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}
. In particular, if
\partial\mathcal{O}=\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}
, for some
k
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{0}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{\varepsilon}_{k})}.

Proof.

The desired bound follows immediately from the continuity of
\varphi
on
\overline{\mathcal{O}}
. The desired scaling follows from the changing of variables in integral, i.e.
|\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}|=\varepsilon^{2}|\Gamma_{k}|
.

∎

Since
\boldsymbol{y}\mapsto\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{-1}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}\in L^{\infty}(\partial\mathcal{O})
, see
[
26
, Theorem 3.3.5]
, we also have

Lemma A.2
.

Let
B
be a convex set containing
\operatorname{conv}\operatorname{hull}\mathcal{O}
. For all
\varphi\in W^{1,\infty}(\overline{{B}})
,

\displaystyle|\gamma_{0}\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}\leq\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\left(\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{1}{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}\right)^{1/2}.

In particular, if
\partial\mathcal{O}=\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}
, for some
k
, it holds that
|\gamma_{0}\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{3/2}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{\varepsilon}_{k})}
.

Proof.

The bounds immediately follows from the definition of the
H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})
seminorm and applying the mean value theorem to
\varphi
:

\displaystyle|\gamma_{0}\varphi|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\partial\mathcal{O}\times\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{|\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})-\varphi(\boldsymbol{y})|^{2}}{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\iint_{\partial\mathcal{O}\times\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{1}{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}.

Replacing
\partial\mathcal{O}
by
\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}
and changing variables in the above integrals, we derive the desired scaling.
∎

Orthogonal projectors associated to
\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}
and related estimates

In this section we will consider the action of
\mathbb{P}_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}_{\perp}
(see Section
4.2
) on
\Omega^{\varepsilon}
for the case when
N=1
. To distinguish this case we will state all the results for the re-scaled domain
\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}
, defined in (
A.1
). We update the notation for the counterparts of
\mathbb{S}_{0}
and
H^{1/2}_{\perp}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{0}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}):=\operatorname{span}\{1\},\quad H^{1/2}_{\perp}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}):=\{\psi\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}):(\psi,1)_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=0\}.

Definition A.1
.

We define the scaling isomorphism between the spaces
L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
and
L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O})
(resp.
H^{s}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
and
H^{s}(\partial\mathcal{O})
,
s\geq 0
) via:

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}:\,H^{s}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})\ni\varphi\mapsto\Phi\in H^{s}(\partial\mathcal{O}):\,\Phi(\boldsymbol{x})=\varphi(\varepsilon\boldsymbol{x}),\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\partial\mathcal{O}.

It indeed holds that
\operatorname{Ran}\left.\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\right|_{\mathbb{S}_{0}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=\mathbb{S}_{0}(\partial\mathcal{O})
, and
\operatorname{Ran}\left.\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\right|_{H^{1/2}_{\perp}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=H^{1/2}_{\perp}(\partial\mathcal{O})
.

The following identities will be of importance later:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}_{0},\quad\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}_{\perp}.

(A.2)

Both of them are a corollary of

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\varphi=1_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{1}{|\partial\mathcal{O}|}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\varphi d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}=1_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{1}{|\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}|}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}=\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi.

Lemma A.3
.

For all
\varphi,\,\psi\in L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
,

(\varphi,\psi)_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=\varepsilon^{2}(\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\varphi,\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\psi)_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}
.

Proof.

By the change of variables,
(\varphi,\psi)_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})\,\overline{\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\psi}(\boldsymbol{x})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

For all
\varphi\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
,
|\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=\varepsilon^{1/2}|\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}.

Proof.

By definition of the seminorm (
2.9
) and the change of variables, with
\Phi=\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\varphi
, it holds that

\displaystyle|\varphi|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{|\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})-\varphi(\boldsymbol{y})|^{2}}{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{4}\varepsilon^{-3}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\frac{|\Phi(\hat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\hat{\boldsymbol{y}})|^{2}}{|\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{y}}|^{3}}d\Gamma_{\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}}d\Gamma_{\hat{\boldsymbol{y}}}=\varepsilon|\Phi|^{2}_{1/2,\partial\mathcal{O}}.

∎

We also have a Poincarè-type inequaility with a constant explicit in
\varepsilon
.

Lemma A.5
.

There exists
C>0
, s.t. for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, all
\varphi\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
,

\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\leq\varepsilon^{1/2}\times C|\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}.

Proof.

We use
\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi=\varphi-\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi
, and
\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi=1_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\langle\varphi\rangle=1_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{|\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}|}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}
.
Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the third line below yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\left|\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})-\langle\varphi\rangle\right|^{2}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\left|\frac{1}{|\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}|}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}-\frac{1}{|\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}|}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\varphi(\boldsymbol{y})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}\right|^{2}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}|}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}|\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})-\varphi(\boldsymbol{y})|^{2}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\text{diam}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}))^{3}}{|\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}|}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{|\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})-\varphi(\boldsymbol{y})|^{2}}{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}\lesssim\varepsilon|\varphi_{\perp}|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})},

where we used
|\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}|=\varepsilon^{2}\times|\partial\mathcal{O}|
and
\text{diam}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})=\varepsilon\text{diam}(\partial\mathcal{O})
.
∎

We also have the following observation, which will be used in the subsequent results.

Corollary A.1
.

Let
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. It holds that
\|\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.1
, we have that
\varphi=\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi+\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi
, for
\varphi\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
. Moreover, this decomposition is orthogonal with respect to the
H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
scalar product, i.e.

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})},

The desired identity follows from the fact
|\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=0
.
∎

Corollary A.2
.

There exists
C>0
, s.t. for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,
\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\leq C|\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}
, for all
\varphi\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
.

Proof.

Since
\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\equiv\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}
, see Corollary
A.1
,
the desired result follows by Lemma
A.5
and
|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=|\varphi-\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=|\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}
.
∎

We also have the following, see Proposition
4.1
:

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}.

(A.3)

Remark 12
.

We will use the following result: given an orthogonal decomposition of the Hilbert space
E=E_{0}\overset{\perp}{+}E_{\perp}
, with
E_{0}
and
E_{\perp}
closed subspaces, the corresponding orthogonal projections
L_{0}
,
L_{\perp}
, and the adjoint projectors
L_{0}^{*}
and
L^{*}_{\perp}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|L_{\perp}^{*}\varphi\|_{E^{\prime}}=\sup\limits_{\psi\in E_{\perp}:\,\|\psi\|_{E}=1}\langle\varphi,L_{\perp}\psi\rangle_{E^{\prime},E}

This is evident by remarking that, on one hand,

\displaystyle\|L_{\perp}^{*}\varphi\|_{E^{\prime}}\geq\sup\limits_{\psi\in E_{\perp}:\,\|\psi\|_{E}=1}\langle\varphi,L_{\perp}\psi\rangle_{E^{\prime},E},

and, on the other hand, for each
\psi\in E:\,\|\psi\|_{E}=1
, the projection
\psi_{\perp}:=L_{\perp}\psi
is either
0
or satisfies
\|\psi_{\perp}\|_{E}\leq 1
, so that
|\langle\varphi,L_{\perp}\psi\rangle|\leq\|\psi_{\perp}\|^{-1}_{E}|\langle\varphi,\psi_{\perp}\rangle|,

and thus

\displaystyle\|L_{\perp}^{*}\varphi\|_{E^{\prime}}\leq\sup\limits_{\psi_{\perp}\in E_{\perp}:\,\|\psi_{\perp}\|_{E}=1}\langle\varphi,\psi_{\perp}\rangle_{E^{\prime},E}\equiv\sup\limits_{\psi_{\perp}\in E_{\perp}:\,\|\psi_{\perp}\|_{E}=1}\langle\varphi,L_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}\rangle_{E^{\prime},E}.

Corollary A.3
.

There exists
C>0
, s.t. for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,
\varphi\in H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
,
\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}{\varphi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\leq C\varepsilon^{3/2}\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}{\varphi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}
.

Proof.

By the density argument it suffices to prove the result for
\varphi\in L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
.
By definition of
\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}{\varphi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{{\psi}\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{\langle{\varphi},\overline{\mathbb{P}_{\perp}{\psi}}\rangle_{-1/2,1/2,\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}}{\|{\psi}\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{{\psi}\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{\langle{\varphi},\overline{\mathbb{P}_{\perp}{\psi}}\rangle_{-1/2,1/2,\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\psi|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}},

as it is immediate from Corollary
A.1
and
\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\geq|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=|\varphi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}
, cf. the proof of Corollary
A.2
. Then

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}{\varphi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{{\psi}\in H^{1/2}_{\perp}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{\langle{\varphi},\overline{\mathbb{P}_{\perp}{\psi}}\rangle_{-1/2,1/2,\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}}{|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\psi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}

\displaystyle=C\sup\limits_{{\psi}\in H^{1/2}_{\perp}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{({\varphi},{\mathbb{P}_{\perp}{\psi}})_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}{|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\psi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}},

and next we use Lemma
A.3
, Lemma
A.4
and (
A.2
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}

\displaystyle\varphi\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\sup\limits_{{\psi}\in H^{1/2}_{\perp}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{(\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}{\varphi},{\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}{\psi}})_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\psi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}}

\displaystyle=C\varepsilon^{3/2}\sup\limits_{{\psi}\in H^{1/2}_{\perp}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{\langle\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}{\varphi},{\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}{\psi}}\rangle_{-1/2,1/2,\partial\mathcal{O}}}{|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\psi|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}}\leq C\varepsilon^{3/2}\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}{\varphi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})},

where we employed as well Corollary
A.2
.

∎

The following observation is straightforward: for all
\varphi\in L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\varphi\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}=\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})},

(A.4)

cf. the proof of Corollary
A.3
.
This is quite trivial in view of

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\varphi\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\psi\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}),\psi\neq 0}\frac{\langle\varphi,\mathbb{P}_{0}\psi\rangle}{\|\psi\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\psi\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}),\psi\neq 0}\frac{\langle\varphi,\mathbb{P}_{0}\psi\rangle}{\sqrt{\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\psi|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}},

as follows from Corollary
A.1
. From the above it follows that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\varphi\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\psi\in\mathbb{S}_{0}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}),\,\psi\neq 0}\frac{\langle\varphi,\mathbb{P}_{0}\psi\rangle}{\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\psi\|_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\psi\in\mathbb{S}_{0}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}),\psi\neq 0}\frac{(\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi,\psi)_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}{\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}}=\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}

where we used that
\varphi\in L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
. Hence the desired conclusion.

Appendix B
Properties of the projectors
\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}
,
\mathbb{Q}^{*}_{\sigma}
,
\mathbb{Q}^{*}_{\perp}

For the results that follow, we will need the following observations.
First of all, by (
4.20
), with
\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}_{k}
being the scaling transform defined on
\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}
, it holds that
\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-1}\sigma_{k}^{1}
. With Corollary
A.3
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{1}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma_{k})}.

(B.1)

Since
c_{k}^{\varepsilon}=\int_{\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}=\varepsilon c_{k}^{1}
, cf. (
4.20
),

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\equiv\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}=c_{k}^{\varepsilon}/|\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}|^{1/2}=c_{k}^{1}/|\Gamma_{k}|^{1/2}=\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k})}.

(B.2)

Proposition B.1
.

There exist
C>0
, s.t. for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, all
\boldsymbol{\psi}\in H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})
, it holds that
\|\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}\mathbb{P}^{*}_{0}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\mathbb{P}^{*}_{0}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}=\operatorname{Id}-\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma}
, on one hand, and, on the other hand,
\operatorname{Im}\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}=H^{-1/2}_{\perp}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})
, thus
\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}=(\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}+\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*})\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}=\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}=\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}(\operatorname{Id}-\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma}).

Therefore,
\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}=\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}.

Using Lemma
4.1
, for
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}=\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\boldsymbol{\psi}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\psi_{0,k}|\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}|(\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon})(c_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}.

With (
B.1
) and
c_{k}^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon c_{k}^{1}
, we have
\|\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{3}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{N}|\psi_{0,k}|^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}^{2}.

∎

Corollary B.1
.

There exist
C_{\sigma},C_{\perp}>0
, s.t., for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, all
\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\varphi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\sigma}\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\boldsymbol{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})},

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\varphi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\perp}(\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\boldsymbol{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}+\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\boldsymbol{\varphi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}).

Proof.

To obtain the first bound in the statement of proposition, we use (
4.17
)(b), according to which
\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma}=\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}
. The desired result follows by the above combined with Proposition
4.5
.

For the second bound, we again make use of the decomposition provided by Proposition
4.2
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\varphi}=\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\boldsymbol{\varphi}+\mathbb{Q}_{\perp}\mathbb{P}_{\perp}^{*}\boldsymbol{\varphi}.

For the first term in the right-hand side, we use the bound of Proposition
B.1
, and for the second one the bound of Proposition
4.5
.
∎

Proposition B.2
.

There exists
C>0
, s.t. for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\psi}\in\mathcal{V}_{G}^{\varepsilon}.

Proof.

Since the set
\{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon},\,k=1,\ldots,N\}
constitutes a basis in
\mathcal{V}_{G}^{\varepsilon}
, each
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}
is supported on
\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon}
, and due to the definition of the norm
H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})
(
2.11
), it suffices to prove the inequality in the statement of the Corollary for
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}
,
k=1,\ldots,N
.

The first bound
\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}
is immediate from the characterization of the
H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})
-norm and
\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})
:

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}=\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}=\sup\limits_{v\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})\setminus\{0\}}\frac{|\langle\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon},v\rangle_{-1/2,1/2,\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}}|}{\|v\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}}\leq\|\sigma_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})},

where the last identity follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the definition (
2.9
) of the norm
H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})
.

Next, let us argue that
\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\leq C_{k}\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}
. With (
4.20
), we have that

\displaystyle\|{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}=\|{\sigma}_{k}^{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k})}=\frac{\|{\sigma}_{k}^{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k})}}{\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{1}\|^{2}_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma_{k})}}\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k})}=C_{k}\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k})},

where
0<C_{k}:=\frac{\|{\sigma}_{k}^{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k})}}{\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{k})}}<+\infty
, since
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma_{k}^{1}=\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{k}|}\int_{\Gamma_{k}}\sigma_{k}^{1}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}>0
, cf. Lemma
4.1
. By (
B.2
), the above rewrites, with
C_{*}:=\max_{k=1,\ldots,N}C_{k}
,

\displaystyle\|{\sigma}_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\leq C_{*}\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\leq C_{*}C\|\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})},

where the last result follows by
\|\mathbb{P}_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow H^{-1/2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}=\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow H^{1/2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\equiv\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow L^{2}(\Gamma_{k}^{\varepsilon})}
and the uniform boundedness of the latter norm in
\varepsilon
, cf. Corollary
A.1
.
∎

Appendix C
Lifting lemma

It can be shown, cf. e.g. Corollary
A.3
and the observation given after its proof, that the scaling of the
H^{-1/2}
norm differ on the space of constant distributions
\mathbb{S}_{0}
and its complement
H^{-1/2}_{\perp}
. As we will see further, this affects coercivity estimates. Thus, we derive lifting lemmas separately for two cases.

Trace operators and jumps.

We introduce the following spaces, to be used later for the definition of trace operators:

\displaystyle H^{1}(\Delta;\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\Gamma^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=H^{1}(\Delta;\Omega^{\varepsilon,c})\oplus H^{1}(\Delta;\Omega^{\varepsilon}),\text{ where }

\displaystyle H^{1}(\Delta;\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle=\{\varphi\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}):\,\Delta\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathcal{O})\},\quad\mathcal{O}\in\{\Omega^{\varepsilon},\,\Omega^{\varepsilon,c}\}.

Let
\boldsymbol{n}
denote the outward unit normal to
\Omega^{\varepsilon}
.

For
\varphi\in H^{1}(\Delta;\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\Gamma^{\varepsilon})
, we denote by
\gamma^{-}_{0}\varphi
and
\gamma^{-}_{1}\varphi
its interior trace and its interior normal trace, and by
\gamma^{+}_{0}\varphi
and
\gamma^{+}_{1}\varphi
its exterior trace and its exterior normal trace. For sufficiently regular
\varphi
, we have
\gamma^{\pm}_{1}\varphi=\boldsymbol{n}\cdot\nabla\gamma^{\pm}_{0}\varphi
. By
[\gamma_{0}\phi]
and
[\gamma_{1}\varphi]
, we denote its jumps:

\displaystyle[\gamma_{0}\varphi]:=\gamma^{-}_{0}\varphi-\gamma^{+}_{0}\varphi,\quad[\gamma_{1}\varphi]:=\gamma^{-}_{1}\varphi-\gamma^{+}_{1}\varphi.

Auxiliary lifting lemmas.

We start with the following set of auxiliary lifting lemmas that we will use in our further proofs. The first result dates back to the seminal work of A. Bamberger and T. Ha Duong
[
4
]
. We recall one of its versions below.

Proposition C.1

(Proposition 2.5.1 in
[
28
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a Lipschitz domain. Then, there exists
C_{\mathcal{O}}
such that for all
\xi\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})
and
a>0
, the unique solution
u\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O})
of the Dirichlet boundary value problem

\displaystyle-\Delta u+a^{2}u=0,\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}~\mathcal{O},

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}u=\xi,\quad

\displaystyle\text{on}~\partial\mathcal{O},

satisfies the following bound:

\displaystyle\|u\|_{a,\mathcal{O}}\leq C_{\mathcal{O}}\max(1,a^{1/2})\|\xi\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}.

(C.1)

In the above the dependence of
C_{\mathcal{O}}
on the domain is implicit, while we need our estimates to be explicit in the parameter
\varepsilon
. Therefore, we resort to a certain type of a scaling argument. From now on let us assume that
\mathcal{O}
is a fixed Lipschitz domain, s.t., without loss of generality,
\boldsymbol{0}\in\mathcal{O}
, and
\operatorname{diam}\mathcal{O}=1
. We define the family of the rescaled domains
\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}:=\{\varepsilon\boldsymbol{x},\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{O}\}
,
0<\varepsilon<1
, and first present a counterpart of Proposition
C.1
for such domains. As discussed before, we consider different possibilities for the boundary value
\xi
, namely,
\xi
is constant or belongs to the space
H^{1/2}_{\perp}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
.

Lemma C.1

(Lifting lemma for the single-particle case)
.

Let
a>0
,
g\in H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})
. Consider, for
0<\varepsilon<1
, the family of functions
(V^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}\subset H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
which satisfy the interior boundary-value problem

\displaystyle-\Delta V^{\varepsilon}+a^{2}V^{\varepsilon}=0~\text{in}~\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon},\quad\quad\gamma^{-}_{0}V^{\varepsilon}=g,

and the exterior boundary-value problem

\displaystyle-\Delta V^{\varepsilon}+a^{2}V^{\varepsilon}=0~\text{in}~\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon,c},\quad\quad\gamma^{+}_{0}V^{\varepsilon}=g.

Then there exists
C>0
, independent of
g,\,a,\varepsilon
, but depending on
\mathcal{O}
, s.t. for all
a>0
,
0<\varepsilon<1
, the function
V^{\varepsilon}
satisfies the following bound:

\displaystyle\|V^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{a,\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}\cup\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon,c}}\leq C\max(1,\varepsilon a)\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\|\mathbb{P}_{0}g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}g\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\right).

Proof.

First of all we remark that both BVPs are well-posed by a standard argument (Lax-Milgram lemma). Let us define a new function on
\mathcal{O}\cup\mathcal{O}^{c}
:

\displaystyle V^{1}(\boldsymbol{x}):=V^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon\boldsymbol{x}),\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{O}\cup\mathcal{O}^{c}.

Next, we relate energy norms of
V^{\varepsilon}
and
V^{1}
. We recall the definition

\displaystyle\|V^{\varepsilon}\|_{a,\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}

\displaystyle=a^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}|V^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}|\nabla V^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x},

and after a change of variables
\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}:=\varepsilon^{-1}\boldsymbol{x}
in the integral,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|V^{\varepsilon}\|_{a,\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left((a\varepsilon)^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}|V^{1}(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}+\int_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla V^{1}(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime})|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\right)=\varepsilon\|V^{1}\|_{a\varepsilon,\mathcal{O}}^{2},

(C.2)

where we used
\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}}V^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})=\varepsilon^{-1}\left.\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x^{\prime}}}V^{1}(\boldsymbol{x^{\prime}})\right|_{\boldsymbol{x^{\prime}}=\varepsilon^{-1}\boldsymbol{x}}
. In a similar manner, using that
\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon,c}=\{\varepsilon\boldsymbol{x},\,\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{O}^{c}\}
, we prove an analogous identity on
\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon,c}
:

\displaystyle\|V^{\varepsilon}\|_{a,\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon,c}}^{2}=\varepsilon\|V^{1}\|_{a\varepsilon,\mathcal{O}^{c}}^{2}.

(C.3)

Let us now remark that
V^{1}
is defined on
\varepsilon
-independent domain
\mathcal{O}\cup\mathcal{O}^{c}
and is indeed
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. It satisfies the following BVP, obtained by replacing
V^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})
by
V^{1}(\varepsilon^{-1}\boldsymbol{x})
in the BVPs in the statement of the lemma:

\displaystyle-\Delta V^{1}+(\varepsilon a)^{2}V^{1}=0\text{ in }\mathcal{O}\cup\mathcal{O}^{c},

\displaystyle\left.V^{1}\right|_{\partial\mathcal{O}}=\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}g,

where
\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}
is an isomorphism from Definition
A.1
.
Comparing the above to the statement of Proposition
C.1
, we see that

\displaystyle\|V^{1}\|_{a\varepsilon,\mathcal{D}}\leq C_{\mathcal{D}}\max(1,(\varepsilon a)^{1/2})\|\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}g\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{D})},\quad\mathcal{D}\in\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{c}\}.

Substituting the above two bounds into (
C.2
) and (
C.3
) yields

\displaystyle\|V^{\varepsilon}\|_{a,\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon}\cup\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon,c}}^{2}\leq C\max(1,\varepsilon a)\varepsilon\|\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}g\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}^{2}.

We rewrite

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}g\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{P}_{0}g+\mathbb{P}_{\perp}g)\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O})}=\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O})}+\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\mathcal{J}^{\varepsilon}g\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-2}\|\mathbb{P}_{0}g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+\varepsilon^{-2}\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}+\varepsilon^{-1}|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}g|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})},

where, in the second equality, we used property (
A.2
) along with Proposition
4.1
; in the last line, we applied Lemmas
A.3
and
A.4
. It remains to conclude by employing Corollary
A.2
to bound
\varepsilon^{-2}\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}g|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\mathcal{O}^{\varepsilon})}
.
∎

The above lemma can be generalized to the many-particle case, for a price of an estimate depending on the distance between the particles (see
[
17
]
or
[
19
, Proposition 4.4]
5
5

5

In
[
19
, Equation 4.20, p. 19]
, the definition of the cut-off functions in the proof of Proposition 4.4 contains a minor inaccuracy. This does not affect the correctness of the main result.
for an analogous result).

Lemma C.2

(Lifting lemma for many particles)
.

Let
a>0
,
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})
and
\Lambda^{\varepsilon}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
satisfy the following interior and exterior boundary problems

\displaystyle-\Delta\Lambda^{\varepsilon}+a^{2}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}=0~in~\Omega^{\varepsilon},\quad\gamma^{-}_{0}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle-\Delta\Lambda^{\varepsilon}+a^{2}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}=0~in~\Omega^{\varepsilon,c},\quad\gamma^{+}_{0}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}.

Then,

\displaystyle\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}\leq c(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}(1+a)\max(1,a^{-2})\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\|\mathbb{P}_{0}\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}+\|\mathbb{P}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\right),

and
c>0
is a constant independent of
N,\varepsilon,d^{\varepsilon}_{ij},a
.

Proof.

The function
\Lambda^{\varepsilon}
defined as above minimizes the squared energy norm
v\mapsto\|v\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\Gamma^{\varepsilon}}
for functions
v\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\Gamma^{\varepsilon})
, s.t.
\gamma_{0}v=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}
. In other words,

\Lambda^{\varepsilon}=\arg\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}):\\
\gamma_{0}v=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}\|v\|_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}.

If there exists
v^{\varepsilon}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
s.t.
\gamma_{0}v^{\varepsilon}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}
and
\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}
, then, in virtue of the above observation,

\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}\|_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}\leq\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\|\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}.

(C.4)

Our goal is now to construct such a function
v
, for which
C_{\varepsilon}
will be easy to compute. The key idea is to define
v
as a solution of a certain BVP in the vicinity of
\Omega_{k}^{\varepsilon}
, and
0
otherwise. This relies on an introduction of certain cutoff functions.

We will need cutoff functions parametrized by two parameters
r,\,d
. First of all, let

\displaystyle\chi_{d}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};[0,1]),\quad\chi_{d}(\rho):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\rho\leq d/8\\
0,&\rho\geq d/4,\\
\in[0,1],&\text{ otherwise },\end{array}\right.

Evidently,
\chi_{d}(\rho)=\chi_{1}(\rho/d)
. Such a function
\chi_{1}
can be constructed e.g. as an appropriate mollification of
\mathbbm{1}_{\rho<1/8}
.
Next, we define
\chi_{r,d}(\rho):=\chi_{d}(\rho-r)=\chi_{1}(\frac{\rho-r}{d})
,
\rho\geq 0
.

With the above definition, let us introduce a truncation function associated to
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{k}
:

\displaystyle\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\chi_{r^{\varepsilon}_{k},\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*}}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|),\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

Note that, by construction

\displaystyle\text{supp}\,\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\subseteq B(\boldsymbol{c}_{k},r^{\varepsilon}_{k}+\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*}/4),\quad\text{ therefore, }\quad\overline{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{k}}\subset\operatorname{supp}\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon},\quad\left.\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}\right|_{\overline{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{k}}}=1,

(C.5)

\displaystyle\text{supp}\,\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\cap\text{supp}\,\chi_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}=\emptyset,\quad\text{if}~k\neq\ell.

(C.6)

Moreover, because
\chi_{r,d}(\rho)=\chi_{1}\left(\frac{\rho-r}{d}\right)
, with some
C>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq 1,\quad\|\nabla\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\equiv\|\partial_{\rho}\chi_{r_{k}^{\varepsilon},\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-1}.

(C.7)

Using the above definition of cut-off functions, let us introduce a lifting
\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{\chi}
of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}
(this will play a role of the function
v^{\varepsilon}
in the beginning of the proof) as follows:

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}:=\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{\chi}:=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k},

where
\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
satisfies the following Dirichlet boundary problem:

\displaystyle-\Delta\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}+a^{2}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}=0\text{ in }\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{k},\quad\gamma^{-}_{0}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}=\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k},

\displaystyle-\Delta\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}+a^{2}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}=0\text{ in }\Omega^{\varepsilon,c}_{k},\quad\gamma^{+}_{0}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}=\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}.

The properties (
C.5
) and (
C.6
) ensure that
\gamma_{0}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\varepsilon}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}
. Let us now estimate the norm of
\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{\chi}
by definition. In virtue of (
C.6
),
\chi_{k}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}
are mutually orthogonal in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and thus

\displaystyle\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{\chi}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\|\sum_{k=1}^{N}\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(a^{2}\|\chi_{k}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}+\chi_{k}^{\varepsilon}\nabla\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left[a^{2}\|\chi_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla\chi_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\chi_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:bounds_on_cut_off}}{\leq}2C\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left[\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}+(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2C\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left[\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}+(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}a^{-2}\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2C(1+a^{-2}(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2})\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}\leq 2C(1+a^{-2})\max(1,(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2})\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=4C\max(1,a^{-2})(\underline{d}^{\varepsilon}_{*})^{-2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|^{2}_{a,\mathbb{R}^{3}}.

Next, we apply Lemma
C.1
to bound each
\Lambda_{k}^{\varepsilon}
in terms of
\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{k}
with the bound
\max(1,\varepsilon a)\leq(1+a)
, and the desired result follows at once by (
C.4
) with
v^{\varepsilon}=\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{\chi}
.
∎

Appendix D
A counterpart of Proposition
4.9

Proposition D.1
.

Let
B:=\cup_{k=1}^{N}B_{k}
and
\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\in H^{5}(B)
. Then, there exists
C>0
, such that the following bounds hold true:

\displaystyle\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq CN^{1/2}\varepsilon\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{H^{3}(B)},

\displaystyle|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq CN^{1/2}\varepsilon^{3/2}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{H^{3}(B)},

\displaystyle\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon}-\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq CN^{1/2}\varepsilon^{3}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{H^{4}(B)},

\displaystyle|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon}-\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq CN^{1/2}\varepsilon^{5/2}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{H^{5}(B)}.

Proof.

The argument follows the same lines as the proof of Proposition
4.9
. Remark that, since
\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\in H^{5}(B)
, by the Sobolev embedding
[
3
, Theorem 4.12, part 2]
,
\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\in C^{3,\alpha}(\overline{B})
,
\alpha<1/2
.

Next, we recall that

\displaystyle\hat{g}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{k}(\boldsymbol{x}):=-\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k})-\nabla\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k})\cdot(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}),\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}.

Using the above identity and Lemma
A.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(B^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\leq CN\varepsilon^{2}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{H^{3}(B)}.

Next, we remark that

\displaystyle\hat{g}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{k}(\boldsymbol{x})-\hat{g}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{k}(\boldsymbol{y})=-\nabla\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k})\cdot(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}),\quad\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}.

Using the above identity, we have that, for
C>0
,

\displaystyle|\hat{g}^{\varepsilon,(1)}_{k}|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}\times\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}}\frac{|\nabla\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k})\cdot(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y})|^{2}}{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{3/2}\iint_{\Gamma_{k}\times\Gamma_{k}}\frac{|\nabla\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k})|^{2}|\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{y}}|^{2}}{|\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{y}}|^{3}}d\Gamma_{\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}}d\Gamma_{\hat{\boldsymbol{y}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{3}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(B_{k})},

which implies

\displaystyle|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\varepsilon,(1)}|^{2}_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon})}\leq C\varepsilon^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(B_{k})}\leq CN\varepsilon^{3}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|^{2}_{H^{3}(B)}.

Let us prove last two bounds. It is straightforward to verify that

\displaystyle\varphi_{k}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\hat{g}_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})-\hat{g}_{k}^{\varepsilon,(1)}(\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{x})-\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k})-\nabla\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k})\cdot(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=2}R_{k,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x})(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},\quad\boldsymbol{x}\in\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k},

where
R_{k,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{2}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!}\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)D^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{\boldsymbol{x}}\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{k}+s(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}))ds
. Evidently,
\|R_{k,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\leq C\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{W^{2,\infty}(B^{\varepsilon}_{k})}
and
(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})^{\alpha}=\varepsilon^{2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k})^{\alpha}
for
\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}\in B_{k}
and
|\alpha|=2
.

Then, using Lemma
A.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\leq C\varepsilon\|\varphi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\leq C\varepsilon^{3}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{W^{2,\infty}(B^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\leq C\varepsilon^{3}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{H^{4}(B)},

which immediately implies the desired bound.

Similarly, using Lemma
A.2
yields

\displaystyle|\varphi_{k}|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\leq C\varepsilon^{3/2}\|\nabla\varphi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{k}})}\leq C\varepsilon^{5/2}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\overline{B^{\varepsilon}_{k}})}\leq C\varepsilon^{5/2}\|\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}\|_{H^{5}(B)},

from which the desired bound follows directly.
∎

Appendix E
Derivation and analysis of the Born model (
2.18
)

The key idea in the analysis of the Born model (
2.18
) lays in rewriting it in the form suggested by (
2.15
) and (
2.17
) and analysing the underlying error just like in Section
6.2
. It is straightforward to see that (
2.18
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}_{B,\operatorname{app}}(\boldsymbol{x},t)=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\lambda_{B,k}^{\varepsilon}(t-|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|)}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}c_{k}^{\varepsilon},\quad(\boldsymbol{x},t)\in\Gamma^{\varepsilon}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0},

where the time-dependent functions
\{\lambda_{B,k}^{\varepsilon}(t)\}_{k=1}^{N}
solves the following convolutional in time system

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}(K_{B,\ell k}^{\varepsilon}*\lambda_{B,k}^{\varepsilon})(t)=-u^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell},t)c_{\ell}^{\varepsilon},\quad\text{with}

(E.1)

\displaystyle K_{B,\ell k}^{\varepsilon}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\delta(t)c_{k}^{\varepsilon},&\ell=k,\\
0,&\ell\neq k.\end{array}\right.

To obtain a suitable convergence estimate, we again work in the frequency domain, and compare the above model to (
2.17
), which we have shown to be convergent of order
p=2
.

Density error

In the frequency domain, the Born model rewrites

&\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon}_{\ell k}(\omega)=c^{\varepsilon}_{k}\delta_{\ell k},\quad q^{\varepsilon,(2)}_{\ell}(\omega)=-\int_{\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{\ell}}\hat{u}^{\operatorname{inc}}(\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell},\omega){\sigma}_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})d\Gamma_{\boldsymbol{x}}.\end{split}

(E.4)

As a reference point, we take (

):

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\omega)\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern We start with the following result about the difference of the matrix inverses.

Lemma E.1
.

The matrix
\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon}

is obviously invertible and satisfies

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\times C_{\operatorname{geom}}(N,\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})p(|\omega|,\operatorname{Im}\omega),

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}-(\mathbb{M}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}\|_{F}\leq C_{\operatorname{geom}}(N,\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})p(|\omega|,\operatorname{Im}\omega),

for all
\omega\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
.

Proof.

The matrix
\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon}
is evidently invertible since
c_{k}^{\varepsilon}>0
for all
k
. Also, by (
4.20
) we have that
c_{k}^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon c_{k}^{1}
, thus
\|(\mathbb{B}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}\|_{F}\leq C\sqrt{N}\varepsilon^{-1}
. Following the proof of Proposition
6.2
, we see that, cf. (
6.28
),

\displaystyle|\mathbb{B}_{kk}^{\varepsilon}-\mathbb{M}_{s,kk}^{\varepsilon}|\leq(4\pi)^{-1}|\omega|(c_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}|\omega|^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}(1+|\omega|)^{2}.

Similarly, for the off-diagonal elements,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{B}_{\ell k}^{\varepsilon}-\mathbb{M}_{s,\ell k}^{\varepsilon}|\leq|G_{\omega}(\boldsymbol{c}_{\ell},\boldsymbol{c}_{k})|c_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}c_{k}^{\varepsilon}+\widetilde{C}_{\ell k}\varepsilon^{3}(\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})^{-2}(1+|\omega|)\leq C\varepsilon^{2}(\underline{d}_{*}^{\varepsilon})^{-2}(1+|\omega|).

We conclude like in Proposition
6.2
.
∎

(E.6)

The error of the field

In the frequency domain, the Born model yields the following scattered field approximation

\displaystyle\hat{u}^{\varepsilon}_{B,\operatorname{app}}(\boldsymbol{x},\omega)=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{i\omega|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}}{4\pi|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{c}_{k}|}c_{k}^{\varepsilon}\hat{\lambda}_{B,k}^{\varepsilon}.

(E.7)

Again, we will compare the above to the approximation obtained with (
2.17
):
